TOOLKIT & METHODOLOGY\n\n## 3.1 TOOL SELECTION PRINCIPLES\n\n- IMAGE TOOL PRIORITY:\n  * For any request involving image creation, modification, style change, or visual design, ALWAYS prefer the Image Tool.\n  * Do NOT describe images purely in text when an image output is expected.\n  * If an image result is required, the Image Tool is mandatory.\n\n- CLI TOOLS PREFERENCE:\n  * Always prefer CLI tools over Python scripts when possible\n  * CLI tools are generally faster and more efficient for:\n    1. Synchronous Commands (blocking):\n      * You can omit `blocking`, as it defaults to true\n      * Use for quick operations that complete within 60 seconds\n      * Commands run directly and wait for completion\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="default" blocking="true">\n        ls -l\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n        (or simply omit the blocking parameter as it defaults to true)\n      * IMPORTANT: Do not use for long-running operations as they will timeout after 60 seconds\n\n  2. Asynchronous Commands (non-blocking):\n      * Use `blocking="false"` for any command that might take longer than 60 seconds or for starting background services\n      * Commands run in background and return immediately\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="dev" blocking="false">\n        npm run dev\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n      * Common use cases:\n        - Development servers (Next.js, React, etc.)\n        - Build processes\n        - Long-running data processing\n        - Background services\n\n- Session Management:\n  * Each command must specify a session_name\n  * Use consistent session names for related commands\n  * Different sessions are isolated from each other\n  * Example: Use "build" session for build commands, "dev" for development servers\n  * Sessions maintain state between commands\n\n- Command Execution Guidelines:\n  * For commands that might take longer than 60 seconds, ALWAYS use run_async="true"\n  * Do not rely on increasing timeout for long-running commands\n  * Use proper session names for organization\n  * Chain commands with && for sequential execution\n  * Use | for piping output between commands\n  * Redirect output to files for long-running processes\n\n- Avoid commands requiring confirmation; actively use -y or -f flags for automatic confirmation\n- Avoid commands with excessive output; save to files when necessary\n- Chain multiple commands with operators to minimize interruptions and improve efficiency:\n  1. Use && for sequential execution: `command1 && command2 && command3`\n  2. Use || for fallback execution: `command1 || command2`\n  3. Use ; for unconditional execution: `command1; command2`\n  4. Use | for piping output: `command1 | command2`\n  5. Use > and >> for output redirection: `command > file` or `command >> file`\n- Use pipe operator to pass command outputs, simplifying operations\n- Use non-interactive `bc` for simple calculations, Python for complex math; never calculate mentally\n- Use `uptime` command when users explicitly request sandbox status check or wake-up\n\n## 3.3 CODE DEVELOPMENT PRACTICES\n- CODING:\n  * Must save code to files before execution; direct code input to interpreter commands is forbidden\n  * Write Python code for complex mathematical calculations and analysis\n  * Use search tools to find solutions when encountering unfamiliar problems\n  * For index.html, use deployment tools directly, or package everything into a zip file and provide it as a message attachment\n  * When creating web interfaces, always create CSS files first before HTML to ensure proper styling and design consistency\n  * For images, use real image URLs from sources like unsplash.com, pexels.com, pixabay.com, giphy.com, or wikimedia.org instead of creating placeholder images; use placeholder.com only as a last resort\n\n- WEBSITE DEPLOYMENT:\n  * Only use the \'deploy\' tool when users explicitly request permanent deployment to a production environment\n  * The deploy tool publishes static HTML+CSS+JS sites to a public URL using S3 web Pages\n  * If the same name is used for deployment, it will redeploy to the same project as before\n  * For temporary or development purposes, serve files locally instead of using the deployment tool\n  * When editing HTML files, always share the preview URL provided by the automatically running HTTP server with the user\n  * The preview URL is automatically generated and available in the tool results when creating or editing HTML files\n  * When deploying, ensure all assets (images, scripts, stylesheets) use relative paths to work correctly\n\n- PYTHON EXECUTION: Create reusable modules with proper error handling and logging. Use -nopgbrk to remove page breaks\n  2. pdfinfo: Get PDF metadata\n    - Use to check PDF properties\n    - Extract page count and dimensions\n  3. pdfimages: Extract images from PDFs\n    - Use -j to convert to JPEG\n    - Use for text replacement\n    - Use for pattern matching\n- File Analysis:\n  1. file: Determine file type\n  2. wc: Count words/lines\n  3. head/tail: View file parts\n  4. less: View large files\n- Data Processing:\n  1. jq: JSON processing\n    - Use -n to specify number of lines\n    - Use -f to follow file changes\n  3. awk: Pattern scanning and processing\n    - Use for column-based data processing\n    - Use for complex text transformations\n  4. find: Locate files and directories\n    - Use for precise text matching\n  2. Combine with CLI tools for powerful searches\n  3. Save complex patterns to files for reuse\n  4. Test patterns with small samples first\n  5. Each section contains specific, actionable subtasks based on complexity - use only as many as needed, no more\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Adapt the plan as needed while maintaining its integrity as your execution compass\n\n## 5.2 TODO.MD FILE STRUCTURE AND USAGE\nThe todo.md file is your primary working document and action plan:\n\n1. Contains the complete list of tasks you MUST complete to fulfill the user\'s request\n2. Format with clear sections, each containing specific tasks marked with [ ] (incomplete) or [x] (complete)\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Before every action, consult your todo.md to determine which task to tackle next\n6. The todo.md serves as your instruction set - if a task is in todo.md, you are responsible for completing it\n7. Update the todo.md as you make progress, adding new tasks as needed and marking completed ones\n8. Never delete tasks from todo.md - instead mark them complete with [x] to maintain a record of your work\n9. Once ALL tasks in todo.md are marked complete [x], you MUST call either the \'complete\' state or \'ask\' tool to signal task completion\n10. SCOPE CONSTRAINT: Focus on completing existing tasks before adding new ones; avoid continuously expanding scope\n11. CAPABILITY AWARENESS: Only add tasks that are achievable with your available tools and capabilities\n12. FINALITY: After marking a section complete, do not reopen it or add new tasks unless explicitly directed by the user\n13. STOPPING CONDITION: If you\'ve made 3 consecutive updates to todo.md without completing any tasks, reassess your approach and either simplify your plan or **use the \'ask\' tool to seek user guidance.**\n14. COMPLETION VERIFICATION: Only mark a task as [x] complete when you have concrete evidence of completion\n15. SIMPLICITY: Keep your todo.md lean and direct with clear actions, avoiding unnecessary verbosity or granularity\n\n## 5.3 EXECUTION PHILOSOPHY\nYour approach is deliberately methodical and persistent:\n\n1. Operate in a continuous loop until explicitly stopped\n2. Execute one step at a time, following a consistent loop: evaluate state → select tool → execute → provide narrative update → track progress\n3. Every action is guided by your todo.md, consulting it before selecting any tool\n4. Thoroughly verify each completed step before moving forward\n5. **Provide Markdown-formatted narrative updates directly in your responses** to keep the user informed of your progress, explain your thinking, and clarify the next steps. Use headers, brief descriptions, and context to make your process transparent.\n6. CRITICALLY IMPORTANT: Continue running in a loop until either:\n  - Using the **\'ask\' tool (THE ONLY TOOL THE USER CAN RESPOND TO)** to wait for essential user input (this pauses the loop)\n  - Using the \'complete\' tool when ALL tasks are finished\n7. For casual conversation:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** to properly end the conversation and wait for user input (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n8. For tasks:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** when you need essential user input to proceed (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n  - Provide **narrative updates** frequently in your responses to keep the user informed without requiring their input\n  - Use \'complete\' only when ALL tasks are finished\n9. MANDATORY COMPLETION:\n  - IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after ALL tasks in todo.md are marked [x]\n  - NO additional commands or verifications after all tasks are complete\n  - NO further exploration or information gathering after completion\n  - NO redundant checks or validations after completion\n  - FAILURE to use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after task completion is a critical error\n\n## 5.4 TASK MANAGEMENT CYCLE\n1. STATE EVALUATION: Examine Todo.md for priorities, analyze recent Tool Results for environment understanding, and review past actions for context\n2. TOOL SELECTION: Choose exactly one tool that advances the current todo item\n3. EXECUTION: Wait for tool execution and observe results\n4. **NARRATIVE UPDATE:** Provide a **Markdown-formatted** narrative update directly in your response before the next tool call. Include explanations of what you\'ve done, what you\'re about to do, and why. Use headers, brief paragraphs, and formatting to enhance readability.\n5. PROGRESS TRACKING: Update todo.md with completed items and new tasks\n6. METHODICAL ITERATION: Repeat until section completion\n7. SECTION TRANSITION: Document completion and move to next section\n8. COMPLETION: IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' when ALL tasks are finished\n\n# 6. CONTENT CREATION\n\n## 6.1 WRITING GUIDELINES\n- Write content in continuous paragraphs using varied sentence lengths for engaging prose; avoid list formatting\n- Use prose and paragraphs by default; 40, margin + 40], radius=18, fill=hl)\\n    save(img, name)\\n\\nmake_follower(\\"snake_follower.png\\", (70, 150, 80, 255))\\n\\n# --- Regular Food (apple-like) ---\\ndef make_apple(name, body=(220, 40, 50, 255), leaf=(60, 160, 70, 255)):\\n    size = 256\\n    img, d = new_canvas(size)\\n    cx = size // 2\\n    # stem\\n    d.line([cx, 40, cx, 80], fill=(110, 70, 40, 255), width=10)\\n    # leaf\\n    d.ellipse([cx, 36, cx + 50, 72], fill=leaf)\\n    # body\\n    d.ellipse([50, 80, 206, 226], fill=body)\\n    # highlight\\n    d.ellipse([78, 104, 120, 146], fill=(255, 180, 180, 180))\\n    save(img, name)\\n\\nmake_apple(\\"food_regular.png\\")\\n\\n# --- Big Food (golden star) ---\\ndef make_star(name, points=5, color=(255, 200, 30, 255), outline=(180, 130, 0, 255)):\\n    size = 256\\n    img, d = new_canvas(size)\\n    cx, cy = size // 2, size // 2\\n    outer, inner = 110, 48\\n    pts = []\\n    for i in range(points * 2):\\n        r = outer if i % 2 == 0 else inner\\n        ang = -math.pi / 2 + i * math.pi / points\\n        pts.append((cx + r * math.cos(ang), cy + r * math.sin(ang)))\\n    d.polygon(pts, fill=color, outline=outline)\\n    # shine\\n    d.ellipse([cx - r, y - r, x + r, y + r], fill=col)\\n    img = img.filter(ImageFilter.GaussianBlur(40))\\n    # subtle dark vignette at edges\\n    overlay = Image.new(\\"RGBA\\", (w, h), (0, 0, 0, 0))\\n    od = ImageDraw.Draw(overlay)\\n    for i in range(80):\\n        a = int(2 + i * 0.8)\\n        od.rectangle([i, i, w - f0) * (t / dur)\\n        env = math.exp(-decay * t)\\n        v = math.sin(2 * math.pi * f * t)\\n        out.append(v * env * vol)\\n    return out\\n\\n\\ndef noise_burst(dur, vol=0.4, decay=8.0, lp=0.3):\\n    n = int(SR * dur)\\n    out = []\\n    prev = 0.0\\n    for i in range(n):\\n        t = i / SR\\n        env = math.exp(-decay * t)\\n        w = random.uniform(-1, 1)\\n        prev = prev + lp * (w - Keeping every \\"did thing A hit thing B\\"\\n * check in one place means the snake, food, and bomb modules never reinvent\\n * the same logic, and the rules are easy to audit in a single file.\\n *\\n * All functions work on grid cells ({x, y}) unless noted otherwise.\\n */\\n\\nimport { sameCell, inBounds } from \\"./utils.js\\";\\n\\n/**\\n * Does `head` collide with any wall?\\n */\\nexport function hitWall(head) {\\n  return !inBounds(head);\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * Does `head` collide with any cell in `cells` (a list of grid cells)?\\n * Optionally skip the first `skip` cells - used for self-collision where the\\n * snake\'s own neck cell must not count.\\n */\\nexport function hitAny(head, cells, skip = 0) {\\n  for (let i = skip; i < cells.length; i++) {\\n    if (sameCell(head, cells[i])) return true;\\n  }\\n  return false;\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * Self-collision: does the head touch any of the snake\'s body cells? {'role': 'assistant', 'content': 'Now the snake module — head + followers with movement, growth, and self-collision handling:', 'tool_calls': [{'id': 'chatcmpl-tool-885edc8fe48e36ce', 'type': 'function', 'function': {'name': 'create_file', 'arguments': '{"file_contents": "/**\\n * snake.js\\n * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n * The snake itself. Unlike a classic snake, this one is a large HEAD followed\\n * by smaller FOLLOWER segments. On spawn it is just the head; eating food\\n * grows the follower list.\\n *\\n * The Snake class owns:\\n *   - the ordered list of body cells (index 0 = head)\\n *   - the current direction and a queued next direction (so two quick turns\\n *     in one tick don\'t reverse the snake into itself)\\n *   - growth logic (how many followers to add on next moves)\\n *   - self-collision handling that respects the active difficulty\\n *\\n * Movement follows classic Snake rules: each tick the head advances one cell\\n * in the current direction and every follower shifts into the previous\\n * segment\'s old position. Growing means we DON\'T drop the tail that tick.\\n */\\n\\nimport {\\n  DIRECTIONS,\\n  areOpposite,\\n  cell as makeCell,\\n  CONFIG,\\n} from \\"./utils.js\\";\\nimport { hitWall, hitSelf } from \\"./collision.js\\";\\n\\nexport class Snake {\\n  constructor(startX, startY, startDirection = DIRECTIONS.RIGHT) {\\n    // Body cells in order from head (0) to tail (last).\\n    this.body = [makeCell(startX, startY)];\\n    this.direction = startDirection;\\n    // Queue of pending direction changes applied one per tick, so fast key\\n    // presses can\'t skip past a safe turn into a self-collision.\\n    this._dirQueue = [];\\n    // Pending growth: number of followers to add over upcoming ticks.\\n    this._pendingGrowth = 0;\\n    // True when the snake ate something this very tick (used by renderer for\\n    // effects, and reset each frame by the game).\\n    this.justAte = false;\\n  }\\n\\n  /** The head cell. */\\n  get head() {\\n    return this.body[0];\\n  }\\n\\n  /** Follower cells (everything after the head). */\\n  get followers() {\\n    return this.body.slice(1);\\n  }\\n\\n  /** Snake Length = Head + Followers, per the spec. */\\n  get length() {\\n    return this.body.length;\\n  }\\n\\n  /** Number of followers (excluding the head). */\\n  get followerCount() {\\n    return this.body.length - 1;\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Enqueue a direction change. Ignores the input if it would reverse the\\n   * snake immediately relative to the LAST queued (or current) direction.\\n   */\\n  setDirection(newDir) {\\n    // Reference direction is the last queued one if any, else current.\\n    const ref = this._dirQueue.length\\n      ? this._dirQueue[this._dirQueue.length - 1]\\n      : this.direction;\\n    if (areOpposite(newDir, ref)) return;\\n    if (newDir.x === ref.x && newDir.y === ref.y) return;\\n    // Keep the queue short: at most 2 pending turns.\\n    if (this._dirQueue.length < 2) this._dirQueue.push(newDir);\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Advance the snake one tick. Returns an object describing what happened:\\n   *   { wallHit, selfHitHead }\\n   * The caller (game.js) decides how to react based on difficulty rules.\\n   */\\n  move() {\\n    // Apply one queued direction, if any, for this tick.\\n    if (this._dirQueue.length) {\\n      this.direction = this._dirQueue.shift();\\n    }\\n\\n    const head = this.head;\\n    const newHead = makeCell(\\n      head.x + this.direction.x,\\n      head.y + this.direction.y\\n    );\\n\\n    // Pre-compute collision flags against the NEW head position BEFORE moving\\n    // the body, so self-collision uses the pre-move body.\\n    const wallHit = hitWall(newHead);\\n    // Self-collision checks the body excluding the neck (index 1) so a normal\\n    // forward step is never a false positive. We pass the current body.\\n    const selfHit = hitSelf(newHead, this.body, 1);\\n\\n    if (wallHit || selfHit) {\\n      return { wallHit, selfHit, newHead: null };\\n    }\\n\\n    // Actually move: insert the new head.\\n    this.body.unshift(newHead);\\n\\n    if (this._pendingGrowth > 0) {\\n      // Growing: keep the tail (don\'t pop), consume one growth unit.\\n      this._pendingGrowth -= 1;\\n    } else {\\n      // Normal step: drop the tail.\\n      this.body.pop();\\n    }\\n\\n    return { wallHit: false, selfHit: false, newHead };\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Schedule `count` followers to be added over the next ticks.\\n   */\\n  grow(count = 1) {\\n    this._pendingGrowth += count;\\n    this.justAte = true;\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Remove `count` followers from the tail. Used by bomb penalties and by\\n   * Normal-mode self-collision. Never removes the head. Returns the number\\n   * actually removed.\\n   */\\n  removeFollowers(count = 1) {\\n    let removed = 0;\\n    while (removed < count && this.body.length > 1) {\\n      this.body.pop();\\n      removed += 1;\\n    }\\n    return removed;\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Reset the per-tick \\"just ate\\" flag. {'role': 'assistant', 'content': 'Now the renderer — the most visual module. It handles canvas resizing, background scaling, and the strict render order (background → food → bomb → followers → head → effects):', 'tool_calls': [{'id': 'chatcmpl-tool-ab729223f4ce7242', 'type': 'function', 'function': {'name': 'create_file', 'arguments': '{"file_contents": "/**\\n * renderer.js\\n * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n * The ONLY module that draws to the canvas. It knows nothing about game rules;\\n * it receives plain state snapshots and paints them. This separation keeps\\n * game logic and rendering independent, so each can evolve without coupling.\\n *\\n * Responsibilities:\\n *   - Resize the canvas to fill its container and compute the square cell size\\n *     that fits the GRID_COLS x GRID_ROWS board into the available space.\\n *   - Draw the background image scaled to cover the board.\\n *   - Draw food, bombs, followers, head, and effects in the exact order the\\n *     spec requires.\\n *   - Provide simple colored fallbacks when an image asset is missing.\\n *\\n * Render order (per spec):\\n *   1. Background\\n *   2. Food\\n *   3. Bomb\\n *   4. Followers\\n *   5. Head\\n *   6. Effects\\n */\\n\\nimport {\\n  CONFIG,\\n  gridCenter,\\n} from \\"./utils.js\\";\\n\\nexport class Renderer {\\n  constructor(canvas, assetManager) {\\n    this.canvas = canvas;\\n    this.ctx = canvas.getContext(\\"2d\\");\\n    this.assets = assetManager;\\n    // Computed each resize: pixel size of one grid cell and the board\'s\\n    // pixel width/height. The board is centered in the canvas if its aspect\\n    // ratio doesn\'t match the container.\\n    this.cellSize = 0;\\n    this.boardWidth = 0;\\n    this.boardHeight = 0;\\n    this.offsetX = 0;\\n    this.offsetY = 0;\\n    this.dpr = window.devicePixelRatio || 1;\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Resize the canvas backing store to match its CSS size (accounting for\\n   * device pixel ratio) and recompute the grid layout. Returns the new layout.\\n   */\\n  resize(cssWidth, cssHeight) {\\n    this.dpr = window.devicePixelRatio || 1;\\n    this.canvas.width = Math.floor(cssWidth * this.dpr);\\n    this.canvas.height = Math.floor(cssHeight * this.dpr);\\n    this.canvas.style.width = cssWidth + \\"px\\";\\n    this.canvas.style.height = cssHeight + \\"px\\";\\n\\n    // Reset transform and scale so we can draw in CSS pixels.\\n    this.ctx.setTransform(this.dpr, 0, 0, this.dpr, 0, 0);\\n\\n    // Fit the square grid into the available space, centered.\\n    const cols = CONFIG.GRID_COLS;\\n    const rows = CONFIG.GRID_ROWS;\\n    const cellByW = cssWidth / cols;\\n    const cellByH = cssHeight / rows;\\n    this.cellSize = Math.floor(Math.min(cellByW, cellByH));\\n    this.boardWidth = this.cellSize * cols;\\n    this.boardHeight = this.cellSize * rows;\\n    this.offsetX = Math.floor((cssWidth - this.boardWidth) / 2);\\n    this.offsetY = Math.floor((cssHeight - this.boardHeight) / 2);\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Translate a grid cell\'s center into absolute canvas pixels (including the\\n   * board offset). Used internally and exposed for hit-testing if needed.\\n   */\\n  _cellCenter(g) {\\n    return {\\n      x: this.offsetX + g.x * this.cellSize + this.cellSize / 2,\\n      y: this.offsetY + g.y * this.cellSize + this.cellSize / 2,\\n    };\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Draw an image sprite centered on a grid cell at a given size ratio of the\\n   * cell. Falls back to a colored circle if the asset is missing.\\n   */\\n  _drawSprite(cellPos, imageKey, sizeRatio, fallbackColor) {\\n    const c = this._cellCenter(cellPos);\\n    const size = this.cellSize * sizeRatio;\\n    const img = this.assets.getImage(imageKey);\\n    if (img) {\\n      this.ctx.drawImage(img, c.x - size / 2, c.y - size / 2, size, size);\\n    } else {\\n      this.ctx.fillStyle = fallbackColor;\\n      this.ctx.beginPath();\\n      this.ctx.arc(c.x, c.y, size / 2, 0, Math.PI * 2);\\n      this.ctx.fill();\\n    }\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Paint the full background: cover-fit background image over the board area.\\n   */\\n  _drawBackground() {\\n    const img = this.assets.getImage(\\"background\\");\\n    const x = this.offsetX;\\n    const y = this.offsetY;\\n    const w = this.boardWidth;\\n    const h = this.boardHeight;\\n    if (img) {\\n      // \\"Cover\\" scaling: fill the board area while preserving aspect ratio.\\n      const iw = img.width;\\n      const ih = img.height;\\n      const scale = Math.max(w / iw, h / ih);\\n      const dw = iw * scale;\\n      const dh = ih * scale;\\n      this.ctx.drawImage(img, x + (w - dw) / 2, y + (h - dh) / 2, dw, dh);\\n    } else {\\n      // Solid fallback color so the board is visible.\\n      this.ctx.fillStyle = \\"#1b2330\\";\\n      this.ctx.fillRect(x, y, w, h);\\n    }\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Draw a fuse/timer ring around a bomb or big food to visualize its\\n   * remaining lifetime. Only drawn when the entity has a finite lifetime.\\n   */\\n  _drawTimerRing(cellPos, elapsedFraction, color) {\\n    if (elapsedFraction == null) return;\\n    const c = this._cellCenter(cellPos);\\n    const r = this.cellSize * 0.52;\\n    this.ctx.save();\\n    this.ctx.lineWidth = Math.max(2, this.cellSize * 0.06);\\n    this.ctx.strokeStyle = \\"rgba(0,0,0,0.45)\\";\\n    this.ctx.beginPath();\\n    this.ctx.arc(c.x, c.y, r, 0, Math.PI * 2);\\n    this.ctx.stroke();\\n    this.ctx.strokeStyle = color;\\n    this.ctx.beginPath();\\n    this.ctx.arc(\\n      c.x,\\n      c.y,\\n      r,\\n      -Math.PI / 2,\\n      -Math.PI / 2 + Math.PI * 2 * (1 - elapsedFraction)\\n    );\\n    this.ctx.stroke();\\n    this.ctx.restore();\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Main draw entry point. `snapshot` is a plain object produced by the game:\\n   *   {\\n   *     snake: { body, justAte },\\n   *     foods: [{ position, kind, elapsedFraction }],\\n   *     bombs: [{ position, elapsedFraction }],\\n   *     effects: [{ type, ... }],\\n   *     state: GAME_STATE\\n   *   }\\n   */\\n  render(snapshot) {\\n    const { ctx } = this;\\n    // Clear the whole canvas (not just the board) so letterboxing is clean.\\n    ctx.clearRect(0, 0, this.canvas.width / this.dpr, this.canvas.height / this.dpr);\\n\\n    // 1. Background\\n    this._drawBackground();\\n\\n    // 2. Food\\n    for (const f of snapshot.foods || []) {\\n      if (f.kind === \\"regular\\") {\\n        this._drawSprite(f.position, \\"foodRegular\\", CONFIG.FOOD_SIZE_RATIO, \\"#e0394b\\");\\n      } else {\\n        this._drawSprite(f.position, \\"foodBig\\", CONFIG.BIG_FOOD_SIZE_RATIO, \\"#ffd400\\");\\n        // Show countdown ring in Hell mode (elapsedFraction present).\\n        if (f.elapsedFraction != null) {\\n          this._drawTimerRing(f.position, f.elapsedFraction, \\"#ffd400\\");\\n        }\\n      }\\n    }\\n\\n    // 3. Bomb\\n    for (const b of snapshot.bombs || []) {\\n      this._drawSprite(b.position, \\"bomb\\", CONFIG.BOMB_SIZE_RATIO, \\"#2a2a33\\");\\n      this._drawTimerRing(b.position, b.elapsedFraction, \\"#ff5a3c\\");\\n    }\\n\\n    // 4. Followers (drawn before the head so the head sits on top)\\n    const body = snapshot.snake ? snapshot.snake.body : [];\\n    for (let i = 1; i < body.length; i++) {\\n      this._drawSprite(body[i], \\"follower\\", CONFIG.FOLLOWER_SIZE_RATIO, \\"#4a9a55\\");\\n    }\\n\\n    // 5. Head\\n    if (body.length > 0) {\\n      this._drawSprite(body[0], \\"head\\", CONFIG.HEAD_SIZE_RATIO, \\"#3a8a45\\");\\n    }\\n\\n    // 6. Effects (e.g. eat pop, explosion)\\n    this._drawEffects(snapshot.effects || []);\\n\\n    // 7. Overlay banners for won/lost/paused states.\\n    this._drawStateOverlay(snapshot.state);\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Draw transient visual effects. Each effect has a `type` and a `progress`\\n   * (0..1) computed by the game. We keep these lightweight and additive.\\n   */\\n  _drawEffects(effects) {\\n    for (const e of effects) {\\n      if (e.type === \\"pop\\") {\\n        const c = this._cellCenter(e.position);\\n        const r = this.cellSize * (0.3 + e.progress * 0.7);\\n        this.ctx.save();\\n        this.ctx.globalAlpha = 1 - e.progress;\\n        this.ctx.strokeStyle = e.color || \\"#ffffff\\";\\n        this.ctx.lineWidth = Math.max(2, this.cellSize * 0.08);\\n        this.ctx.beginPath();\\n        this.ctx.arc(c.x, c.y, r, 0, Math.PI * 2);\\n        this.ctx.stroke();\\n        this.ctx.restore();\\n      } else if (e.type === \\"explosion\\") {\\n        const c = this._cellCenter(e.position);\\n        const r = this.cellSize * (0.4 + e.progress * 1.4);\\n        this.ctx.save();\\n        this.ctx.globalAlpha = 1 - e.progress;\\n        this.ctx.fillStyle = \\"#ff6a2c\\";\\n        this.ctx.beginPath();\\n        this.ctx.arc(c.x, c.y, r, 0, Math.PI * 2);\\n        this.ctx.fill();\\n        this.ctx.restore();\\n      }\\n    }\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Centered banner text for terminal/paused states.\\n   */\\n  _drawStateOverlay(state) {\\n    if (!state || state === \\"playing\\" || state === \\"menu\\") return;\\n    let text = \\"\\";\\n    let sub = \\"\\";\\n    if (state === \\"paused\\") {\\n      text = \\"PAUSED\\";\\n      sub = \\"Press Space or the Pause button to resume\\";\\n    } else if (state === \\"won\\") {\\n      text = \\"YOU WIN!\\";\\n      sub = \\"The board is full. Game owns the main loop and coordinates every other module:\\n * snake, food, bombs, input, audio, rendering, timers, and the HUD. It holds\\n * the canonical game state (score, difficulty, counters) and enforces the\\n * rules that span multiple systems (spawn cadence, collisions, win/lose).\\n *\\n * Update order per the spec, each fixed simulation step:\\n *   1. Input          -> (handled asynchronously via InputManager callbacks)\\n *   2. Move snake     -> snake.move()\\n *   3. Check collisions (wall / self)\\n *   4. Handle food    -> food.tryEat()\\n *   5. Handle bombs   -> bomb.tryHit()\\n *   6. Handle spawning-> counters drive big-food and bomb spawns\\n *   7. Update timers  -> food/bomb lifetimes, effects\\n *   8. Render         -> renderer.render(snapshot)\\n *\\n * The loop is fixed-timestep with accumulator interpolation so movement is\\n * deterministic regardless of frame rate, and pausing simply stops stepping.\\n */\\n\\nimport {\\n  CONFIG,\\n  DIFFICULTIES,\\n  GAME_STATE,\\n  DIRECTIONS,\\n  cell as makeCell,\\n  totalCells,\\n  occupies,\\n  farEnough,\\n} from \\"./utils.js\\";\\nimport { Snake } from \\"./snake.js\\";\\nimport { FoodManager } from \\"./food.js\\";\\nimport { BombManager } from \\"./bomb.js\\";\\nimport { Renderer } from \\"./renderer.js\\";\\nimport { InputManager } from \\"./input.js\\";\\nimport { AudioManager, AudioContextLoader } from \\"./audio.js\\";\\nimport { CountdownTimer, DelayTimer } from \\"./timer.js\\";\\n\\nexport class Game {\\n  constructor({ canvas, assets, hud, onStateChange, onStatsChange }) {\\n    this.canvas = canvas;\\n    this.assets = assets;\\n    this.hud = hud; Returns a random\\n   * free cell or null if none exist (which triggers the win condition).\\n   */\\n  _findFreeCell() {\\n    const occupied = this._occupiedByAll();\\n    const free = [];\\n    for (let x = 0; x < CONFIG.GRID_COLS; x++) {\\n      for (let y = 0; y < CONFIG.GRID_ROWS; y++) {\\n        const c = makeCell(x, y);\\n        if (!occupies(c, occupied)) free.push(c);\\n      }\\n    }\\n    if (free.length === 0) return null;\\n    return free[Math.floor(Math.random() * free.length)];\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Find a free cell that is also at least BOMB_MIN_DISTANCE_FROM_HEAD away\\n   * from the snake head (Manhattan). Falls back to any free cell if the\\n   * distance constraint can\'t be satisfied, but only as a last resort.\\n   */\\n  _findFreeCellForBomb(head) {\\n    const occupied = this._occupiedByAll();\\n    const freeFar = [];\\n    const freeAny = [];\\n    for (let x = 0; x < CONFIG.GRID_COLS; x++) {\\n      for (let y = 0; y < CONFIG.GRID_ROWS; y++) {\\n        const c = makeCell(x, y);\\n        if (occupies(c, occupied)) continue;\\n        freeAny.push(c);\\n        if (head && farEnough(c, head, CONFIG.BOMB_MIN_DISTANCE_FROM_HEAD)) {\\n          freeFar.push(c);\\n        }\\n      }\\n    }\\n    const pool = freeFar.length ? freeFar : freeAny;\\n    if (pool.length === 0) return null;\\n    return pool[Math.floor(Math.random() * pool.length)];\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Combined occupant list: snake body + food cells + bomb cells.\\n   */\\n  _occupiedByAll() {\\n    const cells = [];\\n    if (this.snake) cells.push(...this.snake.occupiedCells);\\n    if (this.food) cells.push(...this.food.occupiedCells);\\n    if (this.bombs) cells.push(...this.bombs.occupiedCells);\\n    return cells;\\n  }\\n\\n  // ------------------------------------------------------------------ the loop\\n\\n  _loop = (now = performance.now()) => {\\n    this._rafId = requestAnimationFrame(this._loop);\\n    if (this.state !== GAME_STATE.PLAYING && this.state !== GAME_STATE.PAUSED) {\\n      // Even when not playing, keep rendering overlays (won/lost).\\n      if (this.state === GAME_STATE.WON || this.state === GAME_STATE.LOST) {\\n        this._render();\\n      }\\n      return;\\n    }\\n\\n    if (this.state === GAME_STATE.PAUSED) {\\n      this._render();\\n      return;\\n    }\\n\\n    // Fixed-timestep stepping for deterministic movement.\\n    const stepMs = this.difficulty.moveIntervalMs;\\n    if (!this._lastTime) this._lastTime = now;\\n    let dt = now - this._lastTime;\\n    this._lastTime = now;\\n    // Clamp dt to avoid spiral-of-death after tab switches.\\n    if (dt > 250) dt = 250;\\n    this._accumulator += dt;\\n\\n    while (this._accumulator >= stepMs) {\\n      this._step();\\n      this._accumulator -= stepMs;\\n      if (this.state !== GAME_STATE.PLAYING) break;\\n    }\\n\\n    // Update transient effects in real time and render.\\n    this._updateEffects(dt);\\n    this._render();\\n  };\\n\\n  /**\\n   * One fixed simulation step.\\n   */\\n  _step() {\\n    // 2. Move snake.\\n    const result = this.snake.move();\\n\\n    // 3. Collisions.\\n    if (result.wallHit) {\\n      this._endGame(false);\\n      return;\\n    }\\n    if (result.selfHit) {\\n      // Hell: instant game over on any self-collision.\\n      if (this.difficulty.selfCollisionInstantGameOver) {\\n        this._endGame(false);\\n        return;\\n      }\\n      // Normal: remove the tail follower. If none left, game over.\\n      if (this.difficulty.selfCollisionRemovesTail) {\\n        const removed = this.snake.removeFollowers(1);\\n        if (removed === 0) {\\n          // No followers left and self-collision occurred -> game over.\\n          this._endGame(false);\\n          return;\\n        }\\n        this.audio.playEffect(\\"bomb\\", 0.5);\\n        this._spawnEffect(\\"explosion\\", this.snake.head, \\"#ff6a2c\\");\\n      }\\n    }\\n\\n    // 4. Handle bombs.\\n    const hitBomb = this.bombs.tryHit(this.snake.head);\\n    if (hitBomb) {\\n      this.audio.playEffect(\\"bomb\\");\\n      this._spawnEffect(\\"explosion\\", hitBomb.position, \\"#ff6a2c\\");\\n      const removed = this.snake.removeFollowers(this.difficulty.bombPenalty);\\n      this._updateHud();\\n      // (Bombs never directly end the game; losing all followers only matters\\n      //  for the Normal self-collision rule, handled on the next self-hit.)\\n    }\\n\\n    // 6. Re-fits the canvas and re-renders.\\n   */\\n  resize(width, height) {\\n    this.renderer.resize(width, height);\\n    if (this.snake) this._render();\\n  }\\n\\n  // ------------------------------------------------------------------ HUD\\n\\n  _updateHud() {\\n    const stats = {\\n      difficulty: this.difficulty.label,\\n      snakeLength: this.snake ? this.snake.length : 1,\\n      followers: this.snake ? this.snake.followerCount : 0,\\n      score: this.score ?? 0,\\n      musicOn: this.audio.musicEnabled,\\n      state: this.state,\\n    };\\n    this.onStatsChange(stats);\\n  }\\n\\n  /** Initialize HUD before a game starts (menu state). */\\n  initHud() {\\n    this.score = 0;\\n    this._updateHud();\\n  }\\n}\\n", "file_path": "snake-game/js/game.js"}'}}]}
